# References

Every technique, method, practice, quote, book, author, film, paper and reference that the project and its predecessors mention, harvested on 2026-10-27 to show how much context surrounds a project, and to credit it ([Credit what we use](agent/rules.md), #332). The owner (Silvio) suggested most of them; the evidence below credits each one only where a file says who brought it, so many stay `unknown`.

## How this list was made

Six read-only agents harvested one source each and a script merged their rows by name; three more agents checked the books, papers, quotes, myths and the most repeated items on the web. The sources:

- `nereus`: Nereus repository.
- `wasp-idp-docs`: wasp-idp (docs/).
- `wasp-idp-rest`: wasp-idp (code, commits, issues).
- `platform-api`: platform-api (the former name of Nereus).
- `early-platform-api`: early-platform-api (a private earlier copy).
- `drive`: raw sessions on Drive (`#pontos/raw`).

The columns:

- **Author / origin** and **Year**: as the source states them; when the web check found different values, the **Verified** column says so.
- **Brought by**: who put it into the conversation, from the evidence (a session file that marks who said what, a "Credit" section, an issue label). `unknown` means the only evidence is a commit by Silvio co-authored by Claude, which does not say who proposed the idea.
- **Verified**: `yes` with the page opened; `known, page not opened` when the checker recognised a classic but did not open the page; `differs` when the page gives another author or year; `checked in the session` when the raw session says it was checked then; `linked in the source, not opened`; `project practice` for the project's own rules, which have nothing outside to check; `not confirmed on the web` when the check could not open or find the text (often a quote inside a book or a report); `not checked`.
- **Where**: the first places it appears, `<source>:<path>:<line>`; `(+N)` counts the other places.

## Conflicting attributions

Places where two sources, or a source and the web, disagree about who wrote it, when, or who brought it.

| Reference | Conflict |
|---|---|
| A mostly complete chart of Neural Networks (Neural Network Zoo) | CONFLICT (dates): Claude says first published 2016 by van Veen and the 2019 version adds Leijnen (from memory), while the chart itself shows 2019; occurrences: 3 |
| Building Evolutionary Architectures | years differ: 2017, 2017; 2nd ed. 2022 |
| Caranguejos com Cérebro (Manguebeat manifesto) | the web gives Fred 04 (Fred Zero Quatro) and DJ Renato L. 1991: Wikipedia dates the manifesto 1991 (source says 1992) and credits DJ Renato L. as co-author |
| Extreme Programming Explained | brought_by differs between sources: Claude (Anthropic), Silvio |
| Forer effect (Barnum effect) | CONFLICT: Silvio thought humility relates to the Forer effect; Claude corrected that Forer is about accepting vague generic descriptions as personal (horoscopes), and the closer one is Dunning-Kruger; occurrences: 2 |
| Kubernetes static pods and mirror pods (analogy for core) | CONFLICT: S25/117:5 says Silvio recalled static pods, but the register at S25/117:19 says the analogy was brought by Claude; occurrences: 3 |
| Lean Inception (book and method) | the web gives Paulo Caroli 2017: Fowler site page is dated 22 Feb 2022 (revision history from 2017); book Lean Inception on Leanpub dates from 2017, not 2018 |
| PACT: action-state communication for multi-agent systems | the web gives Chen Huang, Yuhao Wu, Wenxuan Zhang 2026: arXiv title is What Should Agents Say? Action-state Communication for Efficient Multi-Agent Systems; PACT name not seen in abstract; submitted 3 Jun 2026 |
| Pair programming (owner + agent as a pair) | brought_by differs between sources: Claude (Anthropic), Silvio |
| Progressive disclosure | term popularized in the 1980s-90s per Claude; not checked on the web; origins logged in deck notes: Carroll and Carrithers 1984; Hooper Woolsey 1985; Apple HIG 1992; Nielsen 2006 (differs from the S25/160:9 statement that Nielsen popularized it in the 1980s-90s: possible CONFLICT in dates); also brought by: unknown (handoff, deck notes); occurrences: 3 |
| Sem direção cria uma perigosa ilusão de eficácia / Quantidade não é o mesmo que progresso | CONFLICT: S25/174:5 says book and author were only assumed (to confirm) because they followed file 173; text cut at screen edges; occurrences: 2 |
| Team of Teams / leading like a gardener | CONFLICT-ish: source says the book was not opened and Silvio is to confirm it is the book he read; McChrystal Group article 2025 |
| Team OKR em Ação | the web gives Paulo Caroli 2025: found only as English edition Team OKR in Action (May 2025, preface Manuel Pais); Portuguese title unconfirmed |
| The Strategy is Delivery | the web gives Andrew Greenway, Ben Terrett, Mike Bracken, Tom Loosemore 2018: Wikipedia lists Digital Transformation at Scale: Why the Strategy Is Delivery (London Publishing Partnership, 2018); Bracken is one of several co-authors (co-author list from memory, not shown on the page) |
| Time to 10th pull request (onboarding metric) | CONFLICT: Backstage docs say -55% two years after deploying; Spotify blog says 60 days to under 20 days (>66%); the file flags the mismatch |
| Walking skeleton (thin end-to-end slice) | the web gives Alistair Cockburn 1990s (Crystal Clear, 2004): source credits "Foundry app scaffolding design" but concept is Cockburn (bio lists 1996 meme; Crystal Clear 2004); wiki.c2.com link not opened |
